`safe_eval(seq)` should call evaluate_sequence and handle exceptions:
```
def safe_eval(seq):
    try:
        score = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        if np.isfinite(score):
            return score
    except Exception:
        pass
    return np.inf
```
This uses evaluate_sequence global; no import. This is top-level. It catches exceptions to be safe.

The SOTA LP: We can implement approximately using iterative proportional fitting (IPF) / multiplicative weights. Let's revisit exponentiated gradient for the actual objective (score=max b). It might find optimal/nonlocal. Start from a good skew shape. Objective F(b)=max_k b_k. For scaling invariant, fix sum=1 via normalization. A subgradient direction for each active lag k: h_i = p_{k-i} (valid). To reduce peaks, we can update p_i *= exp(-η h_i) and renormalize. But as noted if active at center and p uniform, h constant, no movement. If multiple active lags, h_i is a convolution of p with indicator/weight of active lags. We can use a smooth surrogate: minimize Φ_τ(p)= log Σ_k exp(τ b_k) (or sum max^q). Subgradient h_i = Σ_k w_k p_{k-i}, where w_k ∝ exp(τ b_k). This gives O(n^2) if done directly, but can compute via FFT: h = p * w (convolution) where w length 2n-1? Specifically h_i = Σ_k w_k p_{k-i}. This is cross-correlation; equivalent to convolution of p with reversed w. We can use FFT to compute h fast. Then update p_i *= exp(-η h_i) (or 1 - ηh) and normalize. This is mirror descent for log-sum-exp. It can find distributions with flattened square convolution.

This is promising. We can implement on discrete p of length n with sum 1. Algorithm:
- Initialize p with a parametric shape (e.g., exponential optimized) or uniform.

- Periodically evaluate exact objective (our fast or evaluate_sequence) and track best.

Implement fast objective:
```
def fast_peak(p):
    n=len(p); L=1
    while L < 2*n-1: L <<=1
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fp*fp, L)[:2*n-1]
    b[b<0] = 0  # tiny
    return float(b.max())
```
Score = 2*n*peak.
But for p sum not1, objective scale invariant; use peak / (sum^2) maybe. We normalize p to sum1. For fast score, use `2*n*peak`.
We can cache L per n.

Exponentiated gradient update in detail:
```
def eg_step(p, n, L, beta, lr, max_iter? ):
    b = conv_peak_array(p,L)
    M = b.max()
    if M <=0: return p,2*n*M
    # weights: z = beta*(b - M)/M? or beta*(b - M)/mean?
    z = beta * (b - M) / (M + tiny)
    # To avoid under/overflow, z min -50
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    w /= w.sum()
    w_rev = w[::-1].copy()
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w_rev, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, L)
    m = 2*n-1
    h = c[m-1 : -1 : -1]  # length n
    # if numerical small negative, set 0
    h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
    # scale to mean ~1
    hmean = h.mean()
    if hmean <=0: return p, 2*n*M
    hs = h / hmean
    # candidate log update
    logp = np.log(p + 1e-300)
    # try lrs
    best_p = p; best_score = 2*n*M
    for cand_lr in candidate_lrs:
       lp = logp - cand_lr * hs
       # clip to avoid underflow, maybe add floor
       lp = np.clip(lp, -50.0, 100.0?)
       q = np.exp(lp)
       qsum = q.sum()
       if qsum <=0: continue
       q /= qsum
       # maybe small floor?
       score = 2*n*fast_conv_peak(q, L)
       if score < best_score:
          best_score=score; best_p=q
    if best_p is p (no improvement):
       # try adaptive very small lr? maybe accept a surrogate decrease?
       # if no improvement, stop or random restart
    return best_p, best_score
```
But the objective may increase before decrease; line search over lrs helps. However if current p is local optimum for smooth surrogate with this beta, no lr improves. We can change beta (focus) or lr schedule, randomize weights by using random active set.

Adaptive: Keep current score. If no improvement over some steps, adapt beta (e.g., increase/decrease), or take a random multiplicative perturbation and continue. We can use **simulated annealing** in log space: small U updates if score lower or with probability? But objective noisy? Use deterministic.

Maybe use a **mini-batch** of weights: Instead of softmax over all b, choose a random subset of top lags with weights. This can escape. For example, select lags where b > 0.9 M (or random top K) and uniform weight among them. Compute h accordingly. This reduces focus and provides subgradient for max constraint. We can alternate.

Implement `compute_h_from_lags(p, lag_indices, weights)`? To use FFT, w is sparse; we can compute h directly O(K*n) if K small. But K ~n maybe; use FFT. We can construct w array with weights on selected lags.

Algorithm loop:
- For each initial shape:
  - Optimize for a time budget (e.g., 30-90s) with a schedule:
    - beta schedule: start high (20), gradually decrease? Or use `beta = 50 / (M*n?)`.
    - lr base: maybe 0.1 to 1.0. We can do a line search each step with lrs [base*0.1, base*0.3, base, base*3] normalized to hmean.
    - After each step, update best.
    - If no improvement for say 50 steps, perturb: p *= exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,size=n)); normalize; maybe reduce beta.
  - Track global best.

But exponentiated gradient might decrease objective only slowly. Need estimate. Starting exponential score1.75, target1.5 reduction peak by 14%. Maybe hundreds of steps. Each step one or few FFT; n=2048 L=4096, very fast. We can do thousands.

Maybe we can do a more direct **max-flow waterfilling** for a target M to see feasibility and optimize M via bisection. Could be more powerful. Let's revisit feasibility: Given M target, find p≥0 sum1 with b_k≤M. This is a quadratic constraint. We can use **projected gradient** on p (simplex) to reduce violations: while max b > M, choose active lag k, reduce p_i for i where p_i p_{k-i} large, renormalize. This is like subgradient. The exponentiated gradient with high beta is similar.

A simple practical algorithm:
- Initialize p (e.g., exponential).
- Repeat:
  - Compute b. Let K = indices where b_k > (1 - δ)*maxb (active peaks).
  - For each active lag k, the contribution to b_k is sum_i p_i p_{k-i}. To reduce b_k, decrease log p_i by proportional to p_{k-i}. This gradient h_i=Σ_{k∈K} p_{k-i}. Update p *= exp(-lr h) / sum.
This is same as weights uniform over active. Use δ e.g., 0.05. If no improvement, adjust δ/lr.
- This is easier and likely good.

Can we derive a better direction? If a peak b_k is too high, we want to reduce the product p_i p_{k-i}. Reducing the smaller of the two? Gradient with respect to p_i is p_{k-i}; proportional update log p_i by p_{k-i} reduces product by factor for both sides when both i and k-i updated: p_i p_{k-i} multiplied by exp(-lr(p_{k-i}+p_i)). It reduces large-small more if small? Maybe okay.

What about **proportional to p_i**? Linear gradient step p <- p - η p_{k-i} would change sum; project. Log update maintains positivity.

Let's test on exponential: Active peak maybe around k≈0.6n? It will reduce p positions involved. It may shift mass.

We can also add **sum-preserving shift**: If peak at k, products high where both p_i and p_{k-i} high. If distribution high at left and tail, peak occurs when left-lag aligns with tail? For exponential high at left, convolution peak maybe left-ish? Reducing the right tail? It will adjust.

Need implement `compute_active_gradient(p, b, active_frac)` efficiently.
Option 1: Build w array: zero length m; for active indices set 1/count; use FFT convolution as above. Complexity similar to softmax. Use FFT always. For active indices many, okay. For sparse active (top few), direct may be faster, but n=2048 FFT okay. Use FFT for all.

Potential problem: If active lag k near edges, h_i only for valid i; many zeros. Good.

Line search and acceptance: We should track best sequence, not current, and maybe if current score worsens, revert to best or use simulated annealing. We can implement:
- `p_cur` maintains a sequence; `p_best` best by fast score.
- If candidate improves `p_cur`, set p_cur=cand.
- If candidate improves global, update best.
- If noImprovementCount > threshold, set p_cur = p_best and change hyperparameters (beta/active_frac). Or generate a random multiplicative perturbation from p_best.

Random perturbations: Since the objective landscape may have many local minima. Use small log-noise: `p = normalize(p * exp(sigma * rng.standard_normal(n)))` with sigma 0.05-0.2. This can escape. But rand noise can increase score; optimizer recovers.

Parametric initial shapes:
For n (e.g., 2048):
- uniform: ones.
- exponential decay: `exp(-alpha * x)` for alpha in [0.5,1,1.5,2,3], x=i/(n-1). Also with a small floor: +0.05 maybe.
- power law: `(1+x)^(-beta)` for beta [0.5,1,2,3], maybe normalized.
- shifted exponential with low endpoint: `exp(-alpha * (1-x))` mirror (same score but keep for local minima? Convolution reversed; score same; optimizer may behave differently? We can include one only).
- cosine half-wave: `1 + a*cos(pi*x)` with a in [-0.3,0,0.3], ensuring positive.
- sums: `exp(-alpha*x) + 0.2`, etc.
- discrete two-level: first half A, second B with比例. But parametric continuous covers.
- Use `height_sequence_1` if length close? It might be a random nonnegative sequence with score maybe >2. We can resample to n and optimize; maybe not needed.

Since time budget large, we can run optimizer on top 3-5 initial shapes at coarse n, then refine best at high n.

But before finalizing, let's consider exact evaluator constraints: It rejects booleans, non-floats, NaN/inf, clips negative, caps 1000. Our p values sum1, all in [0, 0.1 maybe], finite. Good. If we return Python list of floats. Ensure no NaN from underflow? We clip.

Potential performance of `np.fft.rfft` in Python for thousands of iterations: Need avoid overhead of constructing `w` each step? We can use `np.exp` on b length ~4095, okay. `irfft` of product, okay. 5000 steps * 2048 = maybe few seconds? Python overhead maybe 1ms per FFT + exp; 5000 ~5s. For n=4096, L=8192, per step maybe 2ms, 2000 steps ~4s. Total okay. The bottleneck might be direct exact evaluations; keep few.

`_eg_update`Implementation:
```
def _eg_update(p, L, beta, active_frac, lrs):
    n = len(p)
    b = _convolution_array(p, L)
    m = len(b)
    current_score = 2.0 * n * float(b.max())
    if current_score >= np.inf or not np.isfinite(current_score):
        return p, current_score, False
    # determine active threshold
    # Use softmax if active_frac <=0? We'll do softmax always with tau_beta.
    z = beta * (b - b.max()) / (b.max() + 1e-300)
    z = np.clip(z, -50.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    sw = w.sum()
    if sw <= 0: return p,current_score,False
    w /= sw
    w_rev = w[::-1]
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w_rev, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, L)
    h = c[m-1 : -1 : -1]  # if m-1 index; ensure length n
    # In case length mismatch, adjust
    h = np.asarray(h, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    if h.size != n:
       h = np.resize(h, n)
    h = np.where(h > 0, h, 0.0)
    hmean = h.mean()
    if hmean <= 0 or not finite:
       return p,current_score,False
    hs = h / hmean
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    best = p
    best_score = current_score
    best_improved = False
    for lr in lrs:
       lp = logp - lr * hs
       # clip lp lower to avoid underflow; maybe also high to avoid numerical
       lp = np.clip(lp, -700.0, 700.0)
       q = np.exp(lp)
       s = q.sum()
       if s <=0 or not finite: continue
       q /= s
       # optional small floor? If many zeros, add epsilon
       if q.min() <=0:
           # do not floor, zeros okay, but to maintain length maybe add tiny
           q = q + 1e-300
           q /= q.sum()
       score = 2.0 * n * float(_convolution_max(q, L)) # maybe compute max only to save memory? But need b? We can write _convolution_max return max.
       if score < best_score:
           best_score = score; best = q; best_improved=True
    return best, best_score, best_improved
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, budget_s - 20.0)  # leave final time
    candidates = []
    # Try coarse n = 512, 1024, 2048 maybe
    for n in [512, 1024, 2048]:
       if time > deadline-... break
       shapes = generate_initial_shapes(n, rng)
       for p0 in shapes:
          p_best, score_best = optimize_shape(p0, budget_s=... maybe 20-60s, n=n,L=L)
          candidates.append((score_best, p_best, n, 'shape'))
       # maybe combine best coarse to refine next
    # Refine best coarse at n=4096:
    if time allows:
       p_seed = best_coarse resampled to n4096
       optimize_shape(p_seed, remaining ...)
       candidates.append(...)
    # Maybe also optimize direct at n=4096 from top parametric shapes if time.
    # Final exact evaluation:
    best_seq=None; best_exact=inf
    for score,p,n,key in candidates[:20]:
       variants = [p, trim(p, thr), trim mass variants]
       for var in variants:
          if var.size==0 or var.sum()<=0: continue
          # normalize to sum 1? If we renormalize, score scale invariant. We can normalize to sum 1 to be safe.
          v = var / var.sum()
          # cap? values small
          seq = [float(x) for x in v]
          exact = safe_eval(seq)
          if exact < best_exact: update
    if best_seq is None: fallback exponential/flat
    print best_exact
    return best_seq
```

Block search implementation:
```
def block_search(p, deadline, sigma=0.05, block_sizes=[8,16,32,64], rng):
   cur=p.copy; cur_score=fast_score(cur)
   best=cur; best_score=cur_score
   while time<deadline:
      bsize=choice(block_sizes)
      start=rng.integers(0,n-bsize)
      p2=cur.copy
      p2[start:start+bsize] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0,sigma,bsize))
      p2 = np.clip(p2,1e-300,None); p2/=p2.sum()
      score=fast_score(p2)
      if score<cur_score:
          cur=p2; cur_score=score
          if score<best_score: best=p2; best_score=score
   return best
```
This may be effective for smoothing. We can use after EG.

But if objective has plateaus, block search may stagnate. Fine.

Let's design `_convolution_max` efficiently:
```
def _convolve_real(p, L=None):
    n = len(p)
    if L is None:
        L = 1
        target = 2*n - 1
        while L < target: L <<= 1
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fp * fp, L, n=L)
    b = b[:2*n-1]
    # correct small negative
    if b.size and b.min() < 0:
       b[b < 0] = 0.0
    return b
```
But `irfft` returns length L if `n` omitted? Actually `np.fft.irfft(fp*fp, L)` returns L. Use.

Implementation of active uniform weights:
```
def _active_weights(b, active_frac):
    m = len(b); M = float(np.max(b))
    if M <= 0: return None
    # Ensure at least 1 index. We may use top indices by threshold to avoid sorting if active_frac small.
    thresh = M * (1.0 - active_frac)
    active = np.where(b >= thresh)[0]
    if active.size == 0:
       active = np.array([int(np.argmax(b))])
    if active.size > m: # no
       pass
    w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    w[active] = 1.0 / active.size
    return w
```
If active_frac too small, top 1? For active_frac=0.01, threshold 0.99M, likely few lags. Good.

Resample best coarse to larger n:
```
def _resample(p_old, n_new):
   x_old = np.linspace(0,1,len(p_old))
   x_new = np.linspace(0,1,n_new)
   p_new = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p_old)
   p_new = np.maximum(p_new, 1e-300)
   p_new /= p_new.sum()
   return p_new
```

Random perturbation on log p:
```
def _perturb_log(p, sigma, rng):
   logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
   logp += rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=p.size)
   q = np.exp(np.clip(logp, -700, 700))
   s=q.sum(); if not finite or s<=0: return p
   q/=s
   return q
```
Use when stuck: try several sigma, if any improves score from p_best, set p_cur to it.

To allow trimming, don't floor too high. Use 1e-300.
- In `_optimize_shape`, p_cur may have all positives. Use `p_best = p0.copy()`.
- `_fast_score(p, L=None)` returns `2.0 * float(len(p)) * float(np.max(_convolution_array(p,L)))` if p sum? But score should use actual sum if not normalized. We normalize always, but for safety:
```
s = p.sum()
if s<=0: return inf
peak = max(conv(p,p)) # direct sum^2 scale? Actually conv scales with s^2. score=2n*peak/s^2.
return 2*n*peak/(s*s)
```
This makes objective scale invariant and can handle non-normalized. Use this. For p normalized, same. It adds sum cost. Good.

if L None: compute
b = np.fft.irfft(np.fft.rfft(q,L)**2, L)[:2*n-1]
if not finite: return inf? return max with nan_to_num.
return float(np.max(b))
```
If FFT yields tiny negatives, max unaffected (unless all negative due error; nonnegative true positive). Use `np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)`. But if overflow? p sum1 values small, no.

Time budget for `_optimize_shape`: If p0 fast score already very good, we can still optimize. For each stage, call with `budget_s`. It returns best p and score.

Candidate list: To avoid storing many identical, inside `propose_candidate`, after each optimize, append `(score, p.copy(), n)`. At final sort by fast score and evaluate top.

Block search refine: After collecting main candidates, take top coarse/fine and run block_search for remaining time before final. Maybe implement `_block_search(p, deadline, rng, L)`.

Block search implementation detail:
- We can generate candidate by multiplicative perturbation of a random block or entire vector small. Use `sigma` decreasing.
- Instead of full conv for each, use FFT.
- Stop when time. Track best.
```
def _block_search(p, budget_s, rng, L):
   cur=p.copy(); cur_score=_fast_score(p,L)
   best=p.copy(); best_score=cur_score
   deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
   block_sizes = [4,8,16,32,64,128]
   sigma = 0.05
   while time < deadline:
      sz = block_sizes[rng.integers(len(block_sizes))]
      if sz >= n: sz = max(1,n//2)
      st = rng.integers(0, n-sz+1)
      cand = cur.copy()
      cand[st:st+sz] *= np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, sz))
      cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-300); cand /= cand.sum()
      sc = _fast_score(cand,L)
      if sc < cur_score:
          cur=cand; cur_score=sc
          if sc < best_score: best_score=sc; best=cand.copy(); sigma = max(0.005, sigma*0.95)
          else: sigma = min(0.2, sigma*1.05)
      else:
          sigma = max(0.005, sigma*0.9 if sigma>0.02 else sigma)
          # occasional global small noise
      if rng.random()<0.1:
          cand = _perturb_log(cur, 0.02, rng); sc=...
   return best, best_score
```
This may waste time but okay.

import numpy as np

Helper functions:

`def _next_power_of_two(m):`
```
if m <=1: return 1
r=1
while r<m: r <<=1
return r
```

`def _fft_convolution(p, L):`
```
n = len(p)
if L is None: L = _next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
b = np.fft.irfft(fp * fp, L)
b = b[:2*n-1]
b = np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
# tiny negative to zero optional
if b.size and b.min() < 0:
   b[b < 0] = 0.0
return b
```

`def _ff_max_conv(p, L):`
```
b = _fft_convolution(p, L)
if b.size ==0: return np.inf
return float(np.max(b)) if np.isfinite(b).all() else np.inf
```
Actually if b has nan, nan_to_num.

`def _fast_score(p, L=None):`
```
p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
if p.size ==0: return np.inf
s = float(np.sum(p))
if not np.isfinite(s) or s <=0: return np.inf
if L is None: L = _next_power_of_two(2*p.size-1)
peak = _fft_convolution(p, L).max()
if not np.isfinite(peak) or peak <0:
   # maybe compute direct for small?
   if p.size <= 100: peak = float(np.max(np.convolve(p,p)))
   else: return np.inf
score = 2.0 * len(p) * float(peak) / (s*s)
return score if np.isfinite(score) else np.inf
```
Using FFT for score; for p small, direct fallback if not finite. For finite, okay.

`def _normalize_positive(p, floor=0.0):`
```
p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
p[p < 0] = 0
s = p.sum()
if s <=0:
   p = np.ones_like(p)
else:
   p = p/s
if floor >0:
   p = np.maximum(p, floor)
   p /= p.sum()
return p
```

`def _make_exp_shape(n, alpha, floor=0.0):`
```
x = np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n)
p = np.exp(-alpha*x)
if floor>0: p = p + floor
return _normalize_positive(p)
```
Mirror not needed.

`def _make_power_shape(n, beta):`
`p=(1+x)**(-beta)`

`def _make_cos_shape(n,a):`
`p=1.0 + a*np.cos(np.pi*x) + 1e-6`
Ensure positive: max.

`def _make_random_smooth(n, rng):`
```
p = np.ones(n)
m = int(rng.integers(1,4))
x = np.linspace(0,1,n)
for _ in range(m):
    f = float(rng.uniform(0.3,6.0))
    ph = float(rng.uniform(0,2*np.pi))
    amp = float(rng.uniform(0.05,0.45))
    p *= 1.0 + amp*np.cos(2*np.pi*f*x + ph)
return _normalize_positive(p)
```
Could be negative if amp<1 because product of (1+0.45 cos) remains positive? Each factor min 0.55, positive. Good.

`def _make_height_shape(n):`
```
hs = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
if hs is None: return None
arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
arr = np.maximum(arr,0)
if arr.size ==0: return None
if arr.size != n:
   x_old=np.linspace(0,1,arr.size); x_new=np.linspace(0,1,n)
   arr = np.interp(x_new, x_old, arr)
return _normalize_positive(arr)
```

def _rank_and_select(shapes, k, L):

`def _resample(p, n_new):`
```
p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
n_old = p.size
if n_old == n_new: return p
x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_old)
x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,n_new)
q=np.interp(x_new, x_old, p)
q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
q /= q.sum()
return q
```

`def _eg_update(p, L, active_frac):`
No rng. Uses line search. We need a deterministic list of multipliers. Let's set:
```
def _eg_update(p, L, active_frac):
    n=len(p); s=p.sum(); if s <=0: return p, inf
    b = _fft_convolution(p, L)
    Mb = float(np.max(b)) if b.size else inf
    current_score = 2.0*n*Mb/(s*s)
    if not finite current_score: return p,current_score
    m=b.size
    kmax = max(1, int(np.ceil(m * active_frac)))
    # threshold
    thresh = Mb * max(0.0, 1.0 - active_frac - 1e-3) # small
    active = np.where(b >= thresh)[0]
    if active.size == 0:
        active = np.array([int(np.argmax(b))])
    elif active.size > m//2 and kmax < m//2:
        # to avoid too many, use top kmax via argpartition
    else:
        if active.size < kmax and kmax < m:
             part = np.argpartition(b, -kmax)[-kmax:]
             active = part
    # If active huge due flat, use top kmax maybe:
    if active.size > max(kmax, 10):
        if active.size > m // 3:
            part_idx = np.argpartition(b, -kmax)[-kmax:]
            active = part_idx
    w = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    w[active] = 1.0 / float(active.size)
    w_rev = w[::-1].copy()
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w_rev, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, L)
    h = c[m-1 : -1 : -1].copy()
    if h.size != n:
        if h.size < n:
            h = np.pad(h, (0, n-h.size))
        else:
            h = h[:n]
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    h[h<0] = 0.0
    hmean = float(np.mean(h))
    if not finite or hmean <= 1e-300:
        return p, current_score
    hs = h / hmean
    rmax = float(np.max(hs))
    if not finite or rmax <= 0: return p,current_score
    base = 1.0 / max(1.0, rmax)
    lrs = [0.05*base, 0.1*base, 0.3*base, 1.0*base, 2.0*base]
    # Ensure unique positive
    best_p=p; best_score=current_score
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    for lr in lrs:
        if lr <=0 or not finite: continue
        lp = logp - lr * hs
        # To prevent all underflow, clip lower -700, upper maybe 0? If p sum1, log max ~0 for max p; update negative so <=0 except maybe if hs negative no.
        lp = np.clip(lp, -700.0, 50.0)
        q = np.exp(lp)
        qs = float(np.sum(q))
        if not finite qs or qs <=0: continue
        q /= qs
        sc = _fast_score(q, L)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc; best_p = q
    return best_p, best_score
```
The `active.size > max(kmax,10)` logic might use argpartition too often. Sorting/argpartition cost okay but we can simplify:
```
if active.size < max(1, kmax//2):
   idx = np.argpartition(b, -kmax)[-kmax:]
   active = idx
elif active.size > max(kmax*2, 100):

`def _perturb_log(p, sigma, rng):`
```
p=np.asarray(p)
logp=np.log(np.maximum(p,1e-300))
logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=p.size)
q=np.exp(np.clip(logp,-700,700))
s=q.sum()
if s<=0 or not finite: return p
return q/s
```

`def _optimize_shape(p0, budget_s, rng, collect=None):`
```
p0 = _normalize_positive(p0)
n=len(p0); L=_next_power_of_two(2*n-1)
p_best=p0.copy(); score_best=_fast_score(p0,L)
p_cur=p0.copy(); score_cur=score_best
deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
active_fracs = [0.25,0.12,0.05,0.02,0.4,0.18,0.08,0.03]
ai=0; stagnate=0
while time.monotonic() < deadline:
   if time.monotonic() > deadline - 1: break
   af = active_fracs[ai % len(active_fracs)]
   p_new, sc_new = _eg_update(p_cur, L, af)
   if np.isfinite(sc_new) and sc_new < score_cur - 1e-12:
       p_cur=p_new; score_cur=sc_new
       if sc_new < score_best:
           score_best=sc_new; p_best=p_new.copy()
           if collect is not None:
               collect.append((sc_new, p_new.copy(), n))
       stagnate=0
       # occasionally shift active frac
       if stagnate ==0 and rng.random()<0.2:
           ai = int(rng.integers(len(active_fracs)))
   else:
       stagnate +=1
       if stagnate % 10 == 0:
           ai = (ai + 1) % len(active_fracs)
       if stagnate > 80:
           # reset to best and perturb
           p_cur = p_best.copy(); score_cur = score_best
           improved=False
           for sig in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4):
               pp = _perturb_log(p_best, sig, rng)
               sp = _fast_score(pp, L)
               if np.isfinite(sp) and sp < score_best * (1.0 + 1e-5):
                   p_cur=pp; score_cur=sp
                   if sp < score_best:
                       score_best=sp; p_best=pp.copy(); improved=True
                   break
           stagnate=0
           ai = int(rng.integers(len(active_fracs)))
return p_best, score_best
```
But if a perturbation improves score_cur but not best (within 1e-5), p_cur changed. Good.

After EG, maybe block search in same budget? The optimize budget could be split: 70% EG, 30% block. But we can call block separately from propose candidate.

`def _block_search(p, budget_s, rng, L):`
We'll include.
```
def _block_search(p, budget_s, rng, L):
    p = np.asarray(p); best=p.copy(); best_score=_fast_score(p,L)
    cur=best.copy(); cur_score=best_score
    deadline=time.monotonic()+budget_s
    n=cur.size
    block_sizes = [4,8,16,32,64,128]
    sigma=0.05
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
       sz = int(block_sizes[int(rng.integers(len(block_sizes)))])
       if sz >= n:
          sz = max(1, n//2)
       st = int(rng.integers(0, n-sz+1))
       cand=cur.copy()
       mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=sz))
       cand[st:st+sz] *= mult
       cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-300)
       cs = float(np.sum(cand))
       if cs <=0 or not finite: continue
       cand /= cs
       sc = _fast_score(cand,L)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score:
          cur=cand; cur_score=sc
          if sc < best_score:
             best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc
             sigma = max(0.005, sigma*0.95)
          else:
             sigma = min(0.25, sigma*1.05)
       else:
          sigma = max(0.005, sigma*0.97)
          if rng.random() < 0.15:
             cand = _perturb_log(cur, sigma, rng)
             sc = _fast_score(cand,L)
             if sc < cur_score:
                cur=cand; cur_score=sc
                if sc < best_score: best=cand.copy(); best_score=sc
    return best, best_score
```
This uses random noise full vector sometimes.

`def _trim_variants(p):`
```
vars=[p]
# trim leading/trailing below thresholds relative max
for eps in [1e-12,1e-10,1e-8]:
   mask = p > eps*p.max()
   if mask.any():
      idxs = np.where(mask)[0]
      sub = p[idxs[0]:idxs[-1]+1]
      if sub.size >=2: vars.append(sub)
# fixed cuts
n=p.size
for f in (0.0,0.02,0.05,0.10,0.20):
   l = int(n*f); r = int(n*f)
   if n-l-r >= 10:
      sub=p[l:n-r]
      vars.append(sub)
# drop one side only
for f in (0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4):
   l=int(n*f); r=0; if n-l>=10 append p[l:]
   l=0; r=int(n*f); if r<n and n-r>=10 append p[:n-r]
# central mass windows: choose fractions of length 95,99,99.9 maybe already
```

```
def _rank_initial(shapes, n, L):
    arr = []
    for idx, sh in enumerate(shapes):
       p = np.asarray(sh, dtype=np.float64)
       if p.size != n: p = _resample(p, n)
       p = _normalize_positive(p)
       sc = _fast_score(p, L)
       if np.isfinite(sc): arr.append((sc, idx, p))
    # sort by score only: use np.argsort on scores array
    if not arr: return []
    scores = np.array([a[0] for a in arr], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    return [arr[i][2] for i in order]
```

Define `_span_length(p, thresh_frac=1e-12)`:
```
if p.size==0 return 0

if idx.size==0: return 1
return int(idx[-1]-idx[0]+1)
```

def _collect_initial(n, rng):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5)/n
    shapes=[]
    shapes.append(np.ones(n))
    for alpha in (0.4,0.7,1.0,1.3,1.7,2.2,3.0):
       shapes.append(np.exp(-alpha*x))
       shapes.append(np.exp(-alpha*(1.0-x))) # mirror maybe local asym? Score reversal same, but may help corresponding local minima? Include maybe too many.
    for beta in (0.5,1.0,1.5,2.0):
       shapes.append((1.0 + x)**(-beta))
    for a,b in [(0.5,1.5),(0.5,2.5),(0.5,3.5),(1.0,2.0),(1.0,3.0),(2.0,0.5),(3.0,0.5)]:
       xx = np.clip(x, 1e-3, 1-1e-3)
       shapes.append(xx**(a-1.0)*(1.0-xx)**(b-1.0))
    for amp in (0.2,0.35,0.5):
       shapes.append(1.0 + amp*np.cos(np.pi*x))
       shapes.append(1.0 + amp*np.cos(2.0*np.pi*x))
    for _ in range(4):
       shapes.append(_random_smooth(n,rng,x))
    hp = _make_height_shape(n)
    if hp is not None: shapes.append(hp)
    return shapes
```

Now, implement `_sequence_variants` carefully. To avoid too many exact evals, return list prenormalized maybe.
```
def _sequence_variants(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p,0.0)
    if p.size ==0 or p.sum() <=0: return []
    outs=[p.copy()]
    n=p.size
    maxp=p.max()
    if maxp >0:
       for eps in (1e-12,1e-10,1e-8):
          idx=np.where(p > eps*maxp)[0]
          if idx.size > 0:
             sub=p[idx[0]:idx[-1]+1]
             if sub.size >=1: outs.append(sub)
    # cuts
    for cut in (0, int(0.02*n), int(0.05*n), int(0.1*n), int(0.2*n)):
       if n - 2*cut >= 10:
          outs.append(p[cut:n-cut])
    for cut in (int(0.05*n), int(0.1*n), int(0.2*n), int(0.4*n)):
       if n-cut >=10:
          outs.append(p[cut:])
       if cut < n and cut >=10:
          outs.append(p[:cut])
    # deduplicate by length and maybe first? not necessary.
    return outs
```
This could include p itself multiple times; exact repeated okay.

```python
"""
Search for a nonnegative sequence minimizing
    2*n*max(convolve(a,a) / (sum(a)**2).

The code uses fast FFT-based convolution and an exponentiated-gradient /
mirror-descent style line search over probability vectors.  It tries several
lengths and several analytic/random starting shapes, then evaluates the best
candidates with the authoritative evaluate_sequence and returns the best list.
"""

import numpy as np
import time


def _next_power_of_two(m):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    r = 1
    while r < m:
        r <<= 1
    return r


def _fft_convolution(p, L=None):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    if L is None:
        L = _next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fp, L)
    c = c[:2 * n - 1]
    c = np.nan_to_num(c, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    if c.size and float(np.min(c)) < 0.0:
        c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)
    return c


def _span_eff_length(p, rel_thresh=1e-12):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return 1
    mx = float(np.max(p))
    if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
        return int(p.size)
    idx = np.where(p > mx * rel_thresh)[0]
    if idx.size == 0:
        return int(p.size)
    return int(idx[-1] - idx[0] + 1)


def _fast_score(p, L=None):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    if p.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    if L is None:
        L = _next_power_of_two(2 * p.size - 1)
    b = _fft_convolution(p, L)
    if b.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    peak = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(peak) or peak <= 0.0:
        return np.inf
    m = _span_eff_length(p, 1e-12)
    if m <= 0:
        m = p.size
    score = 2.0 * float(m) * peak / (s * s)
    if not np.isfinite(score):
        return np.inf
    return float(score)


def _normalize_positive(p, floor=0.0):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        p = np.ones(p.size if p.size else 1, dtype=np.float64)
        s = 1.0
    p = p / s
    if floor > 0.0:
        p = np.maximum(p, float(floor))
        s = float(np.sum(p))
        if s > 0.0 and np.isfinite(s):
            p = p / s
    return p


def _resample(p, n_new):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    n_old = int(p.size)
    n_new = int(max(1, n_new))
    if n_old == 0:
        return np.zeros(n_new, dtype=np.float64)
    if n_old == 1:
        q = np.full(n_new, float(p[0]), dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(np.sum(q))
        return q / s if s > 0.0 else q
    if n_old == n_new:
        return p
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_old, dtype=np.float64)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, n_new, dtype=np.float64)
    q = np.interp(x_new, x_old, p).astype(np.float64)
    q = np.maximum(q, 1e-300)
    s = float(np.sum(q))
    if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        q = np.ones(n_new, dtype=np.float64)
        s = 1.0
    return q / s


def _make_exp_shape(n, alpha):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    p = np.exp(-float(alpha) * x)
    return _normalize_positive(p)


def _make_power_shape(n, beta):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    p = np.power(1.0 + 2.0 * x, -float(beta))
    return _normalize_positive(p)


def _make_beta_shape(n, a, b):
    x = np.linspace(1e-5, 1.0 - 1e-5, n, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.power(x, a - 1.0) * np.power(1.0 - x, b - 1.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-300)
    return _normalize_positive(p)


def _make_cos_shape(n, amp):
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    p = 1.0 + float(amp) * np.cos(np.pi * x)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
    return _normalize_positive(p)


def _make_random_smooth(n, rng, x=None):
    if x is None:
        x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    p = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
    comps = int(rng.integers(1, 4))
    for _ in range(comps):
        freq = float(rng.uniform(0.3, 6.0))
        phase = float(rng.uniform(0.0, 2.0 * np.pi))
        amp = float(rng.uniform(0.05, 0.45))
        p *= 1.0 + amp * np.cos(2.0 * np.pi * freq * x + phase)
    p = np.maximum(p, 1e-12)
    return _normalize_positive(p)


def _make_height_shape(n):
    try:
        hs = globals().get("height_sequence_1", None)
    except Exception:
        hs = None
    if hs is None:
        return None
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(hs, dtype=np.float64)
    except Exception:
        return None
    arr = np.nan_to_num(arr, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    arr = np.maximum(arr, 0.0)
    if arr.size == 0 or float(np.sum(arr)) <= 0.0:
        return None
    if arr.size != n:
        arr = _resample(arr, n)
    return _normalize_positive(arr)


def _collect_initial(n, rng):
    shapes = []
    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    shapes.append(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))

    for alpha in (0.4, 0.7, 1.0, 1.3, 1.7, 2.2, 3.0):
        shapes.append(np.exp(-alpha * x))

    for beta2 in (0.5, 1.0, 1.5, 2.0, 3.0):
        shapes.append(_make_power_shape(n, beta2))

    for a, b in ((0.5, 1.5), (0.5, 2.5), (0.5, 3.5), (1.0, 2.0), (1.0, 3.0), (2.0, 0.5), (3.0, 0.5)):
        shapes.append(_make_beta_shape(n, a, b))

    for amp in (0.2, 0.35, 0.5):
        shapes.append(_make_cos_shape(n, amp))

    for _ in range(5):
        shapes.append(_make_random_smooth(n, rng, x))

    hp = _make_height_shape(n)
    if hp is not None:
        shapes.append(hp)

    out = []
    for sh in shapes:
        try:
            p = np.asarray(sh, dtype=np.float64)
            if p.size != n:
                p = _resample(p, n)
            p = _normalize_positive(p)
            if p.size == n and float(np.sum(p)) > 0.0:
                out.append(p)
        except Exception:
            pass
    return out


def _rank_initial(shapes, n, L):
    arr = []
    for p in shapes:
        try:
            p2 = _resample(p, n)
            p2 = _normalize_positive(p2)
            sc = _fast_score(p2, L)
            if np.isfinite(sc):
                arr.append((sc, p2))
        except Exception:
            pass
    if not arr:
        return []
    scores = np.array([a[0] for a in arr], dtype=np.float64)
    order = np.argsort(scores)
    return [arr[i][1] for i in order]


def _eg_update(p, L, focus_q):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n == 0:
        return p, np.inf
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return p, np.inf

    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, L)
    b = np.fft.irfft(fp * fp, L)
    b = b[:2 * n - 1]
    b = np.nan_to_num(b, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    b = np.maximum(b, 0.0)
    if b.size == 0:
        return p, np.inf
    peak = float(np.max(b))
    if not np.isfinite(peak) or peak <= 0.0:
        return p, np.inf
    m = _span_eff_length(p, 1e-12)
    if m <= 0:
        m = n
    current_score = 2.0 * float(m) * peak / (s * s)
    if not np.isfinite(current_score):
        return p, np.inf

    Mb = max(peak, 1e-300)
    tiny = 1e-300
    with np.errstate(divide="ignore", invalid="ignore"):
        z = float(focus_q) * np.log(np.maximum(b, tiny) / Mb)
    z = np.clip(z, -60.0, 0.0)
    w = np.exp(z)
    sw = float(np.sum(w))
    if not np.isfinite(sw) or sw <= tiny:
        return p, current_score
    w = w / sw

    w_rev = w[::-1].copy()
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w_rev, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(fp * fw, L)
    m2 = 2 * n - 1
    idx = m2 - 1 - np.arange(n, dtype=np.intp)
    h = c[idx].astype(np.float64, copy=True)
    h = np.nan_to_num(h, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    h = np.maximum(h, 0.0)
    hmean = float(np.mean(h))
    if not np.isfinite(hmean) or hmean <= tiny:
        return p, current_score

    hs = h / hmean
    rmax = float(np.max(hs))
    if not np.isfinite(rmax) or rmax <= 0.0:
        return p, current_score
    base_lr = 1.0 / max(1.0, rmax)
    lr_candidates = (0.05 * base_lr, 0.15 * base_lr, 0.4 * base_lr, 1.0 * base_lr, 2.0 * base_lr)

    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, tiny))
    best_p = p
    best_score = current_score

    for lr in lr_candidates:
        if not np.isfinite(lr) or lr <= 0.0:
            continue
        lp = logp - lr * hs
        lp = np.clip(lp, -700.0, 50.0)
        q = np.exp(lp)
        qs = float(np.sum(q))
        if not np.isfinite(qs) or qs <= tiny:
            continue
        q = q / qs
        try:
            sc = _fast_score(q, L)
        except Exception:
            sc = np.inf
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_p = q
    return best_p, best_score


def _perturb_log(p, sigma, rng):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if s <= 0.0:
        return p
    p = p / s + 1e-10
    p /= float(np.sum(p))
    logp = np.log(np.maximum(p, 1e-300))
    logp = logp + rng.normal(0.0, float(sigma), size=p.size)
    logp = np.clip(logp, -700.0, 700.0)
    q = np.exp(logp)
    qs = float(np.sum(q))
    if not np.isfinite(qs) or qs <= 0.0:
        return p
    return q / qs


def _optimize_shape(p0, budget_s, rng):
    p0 = _normalize_positive(p0)
    n = int(p0.size)
    if n <= 0:
        return p0, np.inf
    L = _next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(budget_s))

    p_best = p0.copy()
    score_best = _fast_score(p0, L)
    p_cur = p0.copy()
    score_cur = score_best

    q_values = (1.0, 3.0, 10.0, 2.0, 5.0, 8.0, 1.0, 4.0, 15.0, 6.0)
    qi = 0
    stagnate = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.5:
            break
        qv = q_values[qi % len(q_values)]
        try:
            p_try, sc_try = _eg_update(p_cur, L, qv)
        except Exception:
            p_try, sc_try = p_cur, score_cur

        if np.isfinite(sc_try) and sc_try < score_cur - 1e-12:
            p_cur = p_try
            score_cur = sc_try
            if sc_try < score_best:
                score_best = sc_try
                p_best = p_cur.copy()
            stagnate = 0
            if rng.random() < 0.25:
                qi = int(rng.integers(len(q_values)))
        else:
            stagnate += 1
            if stagnate % 12 == 0:
                qi = (qi + 1) % len(q_values)
            if stagnate > 90:
                p_cur = p_best.copy()
                score_cur = score_best
                improved = False
                for sig in (0.03, 0.07, 0.15, 0.3):
                    pp = _perturb_log(p_best, sig, rng)
                    sp = _fast_score(pp, L)
                    if np.isfinite(sp) and sp < score_best * (1.0 + 1e-5):
                        p_cur = pp
                        score_cur = sp
                        if sp < score_best:
                            score_best = sp
                            p_best = pp.copy()
                        improved = True
                        break
                stagnate = 0
                qi = int(rng.integers(len(q_values)))
                if not improved:
                    qi = (qi + 1) % len(q_values)
    return p_best, score_best


def _block_search(p, budget_s, rng):
    p = _normalize_positive(p)
    n = int(p.size)
    if n <= 1:
        return p, _fast_score(p)
    L = _next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(1.0, float(budget_s))
    cur = p.copy()
    cur_score = _fast_score(cur, L)
    best = cur.copy()
    best_score = cur_score
    block_sizes = (4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256)
    sigma = 0.05

    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline - 0.2:
            break
        sz = int(block_sizes[int(rng.integers(len(block_sizes)))])
        if sz >= n:
            sz = max(1, n // 2)
        start = int(rng.integers(0, n - sz + 1))
        cand = cur.copy()
        mult = np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, size=sz))
        cand[start:start + sz] *= mult
        cand = np.maximum(cand, 1e-300)
        cs = float(np.sum(cand))
        if cs <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(cs):
            continue
        cand /= cs
        try:
            sc = _fast_score(cand, L)
        except Exception:
            sc = np.inf
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < cur_score:
            cur = cand
            cur_score = sc
            if sc < best_score:
                best = cand.copy()
                best_score = sc
                sigma = max(0.005, sigma * 0.95)
            else:
                sigma = min(0.25, sigma * 1.05)
        else:
            sigma = max(0.005, sigma * 0.97)
            if rng.random() < 0.15:
                cand2 = _perturb_log(cur, sigma * 0.5, rng)
                sc2 = _fast_score(cand2, L)
                if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < cur_score:
                    cur = cand2
                    cur_score = sc2
                    if sc2 < best_score:
                        best = cand2.copy()
                        best_score = sc2
    return best, best_score


def _sequence_variants(p):
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.nan_to_num(p, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    p = np.maximum(p, 0.0)
    if p.size == 0 or float(np.sum(p)) <= 0.0:
        return []
    outs = [p.copy()]
    n = int(p.size)
    mx = float(np.max(p))
    if mx > 0.0:
        for eps in (1e-12, 1e-10, 1e-8):
            idx = np.where(p > eps * mx)[0]
            if idx.size:
                sub = p[idx[0]:idx[-1] + 1]
                if sub.size >= 1 and float(np.sum(sub)) > 0.0:
                    outs.append(sub.copy())

    cuts = (0, int(0.02 * n), int(0.05 * n), int(0.10 * n), int(0.20 * n))
    for c in cuts:
        if n - 2 * c >= 8:
            sub = p[c:n - c]
            if sub.size >= 8 and float(np.sum(sub)) > 0.0:
                outs.append(sub.copy())

    for c in (int(0.05 * n), int(0.10 * n), int(0.20 * n), int(0.40 * n)):
        if n - c >= 8:
            sub = p[c:]
            if sub.size >= 8 and float(np.sum(sub)) > 0.0:
                outs.append(sub.copy())
        if c >= 8 and c < n:
            sub = p[:c]
            if sub.size >= 8 and float(np.sum(sub)) > 0.0:
                outs.append(sub.copy())

    # Deduplicate conservatively by length and first/last non-zero index.
    final = []
    seen = set()
    for sub in outs:
        sub = np.asarray(sub, dtype=np.float64)
        s = float(np.sum(sub))
        if sub.size == 0 or s <= 0.0:
            continue
        nz = np.where(sub > 0.0)[0]
        if nz.size == 0:
            continue
        key = (sub.size, int(nz[0]), int(nz[-1]), float(sub[0]), float(sub[-1]))
        if key not in seen:
            seen.add(key)
            final.append(sub)
        if len(final) >= 18:
            break
    return final


def _safe_eval(seq):
    try:
        score = float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
        if np.isfinite(score):
            return score
    except Exception:
        pass
    return np.inf


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """Run a time-bounded search and return a valid nonnegative list."""
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed))
    budget = float(budget_s)
    start_time = time.monotonic()

    # Leave a conservative final-evaluation margin.
    final_reserve = max(90.0, 0.10 * max(10.0, budget))
    total_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(30.0, budget - 5.0)
    work_deadline = max(time.monotonic() + 10.0, total_deadline - final_reserve)

    candidates = []

    def add_candidate(p, score, n):
        try:
            p2 = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
            if p2.size and np.isfinite(score):
                candidates.append((float(score), p2, int(p2.size)))
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Guaranteed fallback shapes.
    for nfb, alf in ((512, 1.0), (1024, 1.25), (2048, 1.4), (4096, 1.35)):
        if time.monotonic() > work_deadline - 5.0:
            break
        try:
            pf = _make_exp_shape(nfb, alf)
            scf = _fast_score(pf)
            add_candidate(pf, scf, nfb)
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Stages: length, relative budget weight, number of starts.
    stage_defs = (
        (64, 0.035, 3),
        (256, 0.075, 3),
        (1024, 0.185, 3),
        (4096, 0.475, 2),
        (8192, 0.230, 2),
    )
    total_weight = float(sum(w for _, w, _ in stage_defs))
    available = max(0.0, work_deadline - time.monotonic())

    refine_seed = None

    for n, weight, n_starts in stage_defs:
        stage_deadline = time.monotonic()
        stage_budget = max(5.0, (available * weight / total_weight) if available > 0 else 0.0)
        if stage_budget < 5.0:
            continue
        stage_deadline += stage_budget
        if stage_deadline > work_deadline:
            stage_deadline = work_deadline
        if time.monotonic() >= stage_deadline:
            break

        L = _next_power_of_two(2 * n - 1)
        starts = _collect_initial(n, rng)
        if refine_seed is not None:
            starts.insert(0, _resample(refine_seed, n))
        ranked = _rank_initial(starts, n, L)
        if not ranked:
            ranked = [_make_exp_shape(n, 1.25)]

        n_to_try = min(max(1, int(n_starts)), len(ranked), max(1, int(stage_budget // 5.0)))
        best_at_stage = None
        best_score_at_stage = np.inf

        for j in range(n_to_try):
            if time.monotonic() > stage_deadline - 5.0:
                break
            remaining_starts = n_to_try - j
            per_budget = max(3.0, (stage_deadline - time.monotonic()) / float(max(1, remaining_starts)))
            p0 = ranked[j]
            try:
                p_opt, sc_opt = _optimize_shape(p0, per_budget, rng)
                add_candidate(p_opt, sc_opt, n)
                if sc_opt < best_score_at_stage:
                    best_score_at_stage = sc_opt
                    best_at_stage = p_opt
            except Exception:
                pass

        if best_at_stage is not None and np.isfinite(best_score_at_stage):
            refine_seed = best_at_stage
            print(f"stage n={n} best_fast={best_score_at_stage:.10f}")

    # Polish the best few candidates with a small block search if time remains.
    if candidates and time.monotonic() < work_deadline - 10.0:
        csorted = []
        seen_len_first = set()
        for sc, p, nn in candidates:
            try:
                pz = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
                key0 = (int(nn), float(pz.size > 0 and pz[0] or 0.0), float(pz.size > 0 and pz[-1] or 0.0))
            except Exception:
                key0 = (int(nn), 0.0, 0.0)
            if key0 in seen_len_first:
                continue
            seen_len_first.add(key0)
            csorted.append((sc, p, nn))
            if len(csorted) >= 4:
                break
        ccompiled = sorted(csorted, key=lambda q: q[0]) if csorted else []
        if ccompiled:
            polish_deadline = min(work_deadline, time.monotonic() + max(60.0, 0.10 * max(10.0, budget)))
            for sc, p, nn in ccompiled[:2]:
                if time.monotonic() >= polish_deadline:
                    break
                try:
                    pb, sb = _block_search(p, max(15.0, (polish_deadline - time.monotonic()) / 2.0), rng)
                    add_candidate(pb, sb, nn)
                except Exception:
                    pass

    # Sort candidates by fast score.  We will exact-evaluate the best of these.
    if not candidates:
        pfb = _make_exp_shape(2048, 1.3)
        candidates.append((float(_fast_score(pfb)), pfb, 2048))

    # Remove most duplicate lengths/first-last to keep exact eval affordable.
    unique = []
    seen_keys = set()
    for sc, p, nn in candidates:
        try:
            p2 = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
            p2 = np.nan_to_num(p2, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            p2 = np.maximum(p2, 0.0)
            s = float(np.sum(p2))
            if p2.size == 0 or s <= 0.0:
                continue
            key = (int(p2.size), float(p2[0]), float(p2[-1]), float(s))
        except Exception:
            key = (int(nn), 0.0, 0.0, 0.0)
        if key in seen_keys:
            continue
        seen_keys.add(key)
        unique.append((sc, p2, int(p2.size)))

    if unique:
        scores_arr = np.array([x[0] for x in unique], dtype=np.float64)
        order = np.argsort(scores_arr)
        unique = [unique[i] for i in order]

    # Exact evaluation.  This is the authoritative score.
    best_exact = np.inf
    best_seq = None
    exact_deadline = time.monotonic() + max(30.0, min(final_reserve - 10.0, budget * 0.5))

    max_candidates_eval = 10 if budget > 900 else 5
    for sc, p, nn in unique[:max_candidates_eval]:
        if time.monotonic() > exact_deadline - 2.0:
            break
        variants = _sequence_variants(p)
        # Evaluate in an order that usually tries good trims first.
        for var in variants:
            if time.monotonic() > exact_deadline - 1.0:
                break
            if var.size == 0:
                continue
            v = np.asarray(var, dtype=np.float64)
            v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
            v = np.maximum(v, 0.0)
            s = float(np.sum(v))
            if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
                continue
            v = v / s
            # Very small values are fine; also cap for the evaluator.
            v = np.clip(v, 0.0, 1000.0)
            seq = [float(x) for x in v]
            try:
                score = _safe_eval(seq)
                if np.isfinite(score) and score < best_exact:
                    best_exact = score
                    best_seq = seq
                    if best_exact <= 1.25:
                        # Very good already; still continue only if lots of time.
                        if time.monotonic() > exact_deadline - 5.0:
                            break
            except Exception:
                pass
        if best_seq is not None and time.monotonic() > exact_deadline - 10.0:
            break

    # Final fallback: return either the exact-best, or the best fast candidate,
    # or a simple exponential sequence.
    if best_seq is None:
        if unique:
            pfb = np.asarray(unique[0][1], dtype=np.float64)
        else:
            pfb = _make_exp_shape(1024, 1.3)
        pfb = np.nan_to_num(pfb, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
        pfb = np.maximum(pfb, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(pfb))
        if s <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(s):
            pfb = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)
            s = float(np.sum(pfb))
        pfb = pfb / s
        pfb = np.clip(pfb, 0.0, 1000.0)
        best_seq = [float(x) for x in pfb]
        best_exact = _safe_eval(best_seq)

    print(f"final_best={best_exact:.10f} final_len={len(best_seq)}")
    return best_seq
```
